## Document 002: 10月企業物価指数 ～前年比＋7.1％、電力や石油・石炭製品を中心に幾分加速～

Source PDF: all report pdf/10月企業物価指数 ～前年比＋7.1％、電力や石油・石炭製品を中心に幾分加速～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Economic Indicators

2026 年 11 月 12 日

1/2 
10 月企業物価指数 
～前年比＋7.1％、電力や石油・石炭製品を中心に幾分加速～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  阿原 健一郎 
（℡：050-5470-4868）

1. 国内企業物価指数は幾分加速 
 本日公表された 10 月の国内企業物価指数は前年比＋7.1％と、前月（同＋6.8％）から 
伸びが幾分加速した。前年比寄与度で内訳を見ると、玄米や豚肉などの「農林水産物」のマイナ
ス寄与が前月から拡大した一方、「電力・都市ガス・水道」や「石油・石炭製品」、「化学製品」の
プラス寄与が前月から拡大し、ヘッドラインを押し上げた（図表１）。 
 背景には、原油やナフサの価格上昇が石油製品や化学製品へ波及していることに加え、電力に
おいても、過去の輸入燃料価格の上昇がタイムラグを伴って燃料費調整単価に反映され、全体を
押し上げていることがある。農林水産物の価格下落が一部で下押し要因となっているものの、エ
ネルギー関連を中心としたコスト上昇圧力は依然として根強い。

２. 輸入物価指数はわずかに加速 
 10 月の輸入物価指数は前年比＋24.8％と、前月（同＋23.8％）から伸びがわずかに加速した。
「石油・石炭・天然ガス」のプラス寄与が前月から拡大し、ヘッドラインを押し上げた（図表
２）。 
 為替変動の影響を除いた契約通貨ベースでも同＋19.2％と、前月（同＋17.3％）から伸びが拡大
しており、海外の資源価格上昇が輸入物価を押し上げている。輸入コストの増加が時間差を伴っ
て国内の製品価格に波及することを踏まえると、先行きも価格転嫁を通じて消費者物価への上昇
圧力が残る可能性がある。

以 上

## Document 036: 2026-11-13_［みずほ経済EXPRESS］160円台はやはり「円安すぎ」ではない_b6d02f4b

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026-11-13_［みずほ経済EXPRESS］160円台はやはり「円安すぎ」ではない_b6d02f4b.pdf
Report source: Mizuho
Date: 2026-11-13
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1

みずほ経済 EXPRESS

160 円台はやはり「円安すぎ」ではない 
～164 円への円安も視野、介入警戒の磁力が一段安を阻むか～

調査部  主任エコノミスト 東深澤 武史 
080-1069-4919  takeshi.higashifukasawa@mizuho-rt.co.jp 
■ ドル円の安定はかりそめ 
9月下旬以降、1ドル＝150円台後半での小動きが続いたが、これをもって「相場が安定している」と
捉えるのは危うい。チャート上は、上値目途として意識される200日移動平均線が150円台半ばに位置
し、同水準へ向けた円安方向への動きが繰り返された（図表1）。このところの値動きの狭さは、円安
方向を根強く試す動きが抵抗線付近で抑えられた結果とみる方が実態に近そうだ。 
11月に入ってからは、円高になりうる要因が散見されたにもかかわらず、市場ではあまり材料視さ
れていない。米国中間選挙（11月3日）の投開票後には、米国のデータセンター建設投資の持続性に対
する懸念が強まったことで株価が大きく下落した（図表2）。震源地である米国から日本に資金を退避
する動きが生じれば円高要因になるが、ドル円の値動きは限定的だった。また、中東情勢に対する楽
観的な見方から原油安が進展しており、円需給の観点からは先行きの本邦輸入金額の減少を通じて円
安圧力を緩和する要因になるが、この点も現時点では円高につながっていない。 
足元では、米利上げ観測の再燃を背景に再び円安が進んでおり、円安圧力は一段と強まっている。
本稿執筆時点では、心理的節目の200日移動平均線を超え、159円台後半まで上昇している。 
図表1 ドル円レート 図表2 日米株価指数

ドル円

### Page 2

2

本稿では、円安圧力の背景にある要因を改めて整理したうえで、それらを考慮したドル円の推計値
を試算する。実勢値との比較を通じて、足元の円安水準が現在の経済環境に照らしてどの程度説明で
きるのか検証し、その結果をもとに先行きのドル円を展望する。 
■ 現時点でのドル円の適正水準は 1 ドル＝163.70 円 
1ドル＝160円の円安を試す動きは、現在の経済環境に裏付けられたものなのだろうか。この点を検
証するために、東深澤（2026）で構築したモデルを用いて、現在の経済環境から説明されるドル円の
水準を確認する（推計式の詳細や構築過程については東深澤（2026）を参照されたい）。このモデル
を用いて足元の経済環境と整合的なドル円を試算すると、推計値は1ドル＝163.70円と実勢値より円安
になる（図表3）。足元の経済環境下では、160円台はやはり「円安すぎ」ではないということだ。 
モデルが円安水準を支持している理由として最も大きいのが、日本の期待インフレ率の上昇だ。2022
年以降の円安を要因分解すると、米金利上昇や実需の円需給環境の悪化に加え、日本の期待インフレ
率の上昇が大きく寄与している（図表4）。物価が上がれば、同じ金額で買える財・サービスが少なく
なり、円の購買力は低下する。先行きも物価上昇が続くとの見方が強まれば（＝期待インフレ率の上
昇）、将来の円の価値が低下することを織り込む過程で、現在の円相場にも円安圧力がかかると考え
られる。 
実際、日本の期待インフレ率上昇と円安との関係は、データからも確認される。図表5をみると、日
米期待インフレ率格差が縮小すると、円安が進むという対応関係がうかがえる。米国の期待インフレ
率はおおむね安定しており1、日米期待インフレ率格差の縮小は、主に日本の期待インフレ率が上昇し
ていることによる。こうした期待インフレ率の高まりは、賃金・物価上昇の定着にともなう構造的な
変化2だ。期待インフレ率が反転して大幅に低下することは短期的に見込みづらいため、1ドル＝160円
程度の円安水準が定着することは自然だと言える。 
加えて、足元では米金利の上昇も円安圧力となっているようだ。11月以降は、市場予想を上回る物
図表3 ドル円の推計値と実勢値 図表4 2022年以降の円安要因

### Page 3

3

価指標や、ウォラー理事など複数のFRB高官発言がタカ派的と受け止められたことを背景に、米金利
は再度上昇に転じつつある。 
■ 今後は、介入警戒の磁力が一段の円安を阻むかが焦点に 
先行き、推計値（1ドル=163.70円）との乖離を解消する形で円安が進む可能性は十分にある。円安進
行のドライバーになりうるのが、投機筋に積み上がった円買いポジションの巻き戻しだ。図表6は、実
勢値と推計値の乖離を、投機筋の円ポジションに重ねたものだ。これをみると、円買いポジションが
拡大する局面では、実勢値が推計値より円高になる場面が多いことがわかる。とりわけ9月中旬にかけ
ては、日銀の追加利上げ観測などを背景に投機筋の円買い超過幅が拡大した。円買いポジション調整
の余地は十分にあり、巻き戻しの動きが顕在化する場合は1ドル＝164円近傍までの円安進行が視野に
入ってくるということだ。 
こうしたシナリオが実現するかどうかは、為替介入への警戒の持続性に左右される。7月末の日米協
調介入も記憶に新しく、今のところ為替市場は介入への警戒感を維持していると考えられる。11月12
日には三村財務官が円安をけん制する発言を行うなど、日本政府が再び介入姿勢を強めている点を踏
まえれば、160円を持続的に上回るような円安進行は回避されるという見方が穏当だ。 
ただし、リスクは円安方向に大きい。特に、協調介入に対する米国側の姿勢が反転するシナリオに
注意しておきたい。7月当時と比べると、米国政府にとって協調介入の必要性が低下している可能性が
あるためだ。米国政府は当時、日本の長期金利上昇と円安が米国債市場に波及し、米金利を押し上げ
る経路を意識していたとみられる。足元では日米ともに長期金利の上昇が一服しており、日本発の市
場の混乱を防ぐ動機が弱まっている可能性は相応にある。今後、米国政府高官の発言などを機に、米
国が協調介入実施に慎重という見方が広がれば、市場の介入警戒感が和らぎかねない。その場合は、
投機筋のポジション調整を伴う形で、160円を上回って円安が進行するリスクに注意が必要だ。 
以上を踏まえ、2026年末にかけては、米利上げ観測が続き、150円台後半～160円近辺での円安水準
が続く展開を予想する。ただし、投機筋の円買いが巻き戻されたり、協調介入への警戒が和らいだり
図表5 ドル円と日米期待インフレ格差 図表6 推計値と実績値の差及び投機筋の動向

### Page 4

4

すれば、164円近辺までの円安は十分に射程圏内に入る。為替介入が実施されれば1ドル＝150円程度ま
での一時的な円高は想定されるものの、その後は再び円安を試す動きが強まりやすいだろう。

［参考文献］ 
東深澤武史（2026）「160円台が「円安すぎ」ではない理由～「期待インフレ上昇」という新たな構造
変化」、みずほ総合研究所『みずほ経済 EXPRESS』、2026年7月29日 
東深澤武史ほか（2024）「円安が進むドル円の再評価～緩やかな円高予想も、日米金利や円需給を巡る
不確実性は円安リスクに」、みずほリサーチ＆テクノロジーズ『みずほリポート』、2024年4
月23日

1 この間、米国の期待インフレ率は概ね 2%台半ばで横ばい推移となっている。2026 年の中東情勢悪化を受けて急速な原油高が
進展した場面でも、期待インフレ率の急激な上昇は回避されている。背景には、米国の利上げ期待が急速に進んだことにある。
FRB が迅速に利上げを進めることで、先行きのインフレ率は安定するだろうという市場の期待が反映されていると解釈すること
ができる。 
2 実需要因も、円相場の趨勢を考えるうえで重要な要因である。足元の相場変動への寄与は限定的なため本文では詳述していな
いが、実需面の構造変化に関する分析は東深澤ほか（2024）を参照されたい。

## Document 098: 2382廣達 宏遠

Source PDF: all report pdf/2382廣達 宏遠.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Company Report 
公司研究報告 
本報告僅供宏遠投顧內部及客戶參考，雖已力求正確與完整，但因時間及市場客觀因素改變所造成產業、市場
或個股之相關條件改變，投資人需自行考量投資之實際狀況與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任及做任何保
證。非經本公司同意，不得將本報告加以引用或轉載

1 
2382 廣達電腦(Quanta)

報告日期：2026/11/12 研究員：曾智楷(Kevin Tseng) 
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要： 
 3Q26 營收 1 兆 1155 億，YoY +125%，毛利率 4.75%，稅後
淨利 284 億，稅後 EPS 7.08 元。 
 4Q26 新世代 GPU 平台 AI 機櫃預計將進入小批量量產，通用
型伺服器出貨量亦將持續成長，而筆電第四季出貨量則預計將
呈雙位數季減。 
 公司預估 2027 年，伺服器佔總營收比重將進一步攀升至
85%~90%，其中 AI 伺服器佔比將突破 80%。筆電則受記憶體
價格影響，預估營收佔比將降至個位數百分比。

一. 3Q26 毛利率 4.75%，EPS 7.08 元 
廣達 3Q26 營收 1 兆 1155 億，QoQ +7.6% ，YoY +125.2% ，產
品組合部分，受惠於 AI 機櫃 ASP 持續走高，第三季 AI 伺服器營
收呈現季增，但出貨量則較上季略減，主要由於第三季處於新舊
產品世代的轉換期。通用伺服器受惠於 AI Agent 與資料中心需求
擴大，第三季通用型伺服器出貨量表現優於預期。而筆電部分，
第三季出貨量為 960 萬台，QoQ -17%，YoY -24%，主要由於記
憶體漲價影響，以至於今年下半年終端市場需求明顯疲弱。獲利
方面，3Q26 營業毛利 530.2 億，QoQ +1.8%，YoY +56.3%，毛
利率 4.75%，QoQ -0.27ppts，YoY -2.1ppts，公司表示，毛利率
下滑主因高單價 AI 機櫃出貨 ASP 大幅躍升產生稀釋效應，加
上筆電材料成本上揚與規模縮減所致，並非本業獲利能力惡化。
營業利益 322 億，QoQ -6.0% ，YoY +75.3% ，營利率 2.89%，
QoQ -0.41ppts ，YoY -0.82ppts 。3Q26 業外淨利 26.8 億，稅後
284.2 億，QoQ -0.8%，YoY +73.0%，單季稅後 EPS 7.08 元。

二. 4Q26 AI 伺服器動能延續，筆電持續面臨逆風 
公司公告 10 月營收 3532.5 億，MoM +8.6%，YoY +104%，今年
累計營收 3.31 兆，YoY +99.9%。展望 4Q26，公司表示，隨著新
世代 GPU 平台 AI 機櫃於第四季逐步進入小批量量產，受惠於新
產品 ASP 顯著拉升，第四季 AI 伺服器營收仍將維持強勁季增。
通用型伺服器第四季出貨量亦預計持續成長，而筆電第四季出貨
量則預期呈雙位數季減。另外，毛利率部分，由於高單價 AI 機櫃
出貨佔比提升持續稀釋毛利率，再加上筆電出貨規模縮減，預期
4Q26 毛利率將較第三季下滑。預估 4Q26 營收 1.15 兆，QoQ 
+3.8%，YoY +81.3%，毛 利率 4.7%，營利率 2.9%，稅後淨利 290

三. 預估 2026/2027 年 EPS 26.75/31.86 元 
展望 2026 年，公司預估，2026 全年伺服器佔比將達 80%~85%。
其中 AI 伺服器表現優於原先倍增目標，佔整體伺服器營收比重約
75%~80%，佔今年全年營收約 60%~65%。通用型伺服器亦優於
預期，呈雙位數年增，而筆電部分，則受到終端需求疲弱影響，
全年出貨量預計年減 15%~20%。預估公司 2026 全年營收 4.12
兆，YoY +94% ，毛利率 4.81%，營利率 2.99%，稅後淨利 1073
億，全年稅後 EPS 26.75 元。展望 2027 年，隨著新世代 AI 機櫃
於 1Q27 放量，ASP 提升約 50%至接近翻倍，AMD 專案亦將持續
出貨，再加上 ASIC 業務預計明年將有雙位數的成長，預估 2027
年 AI 伺服器營收將維持高雙位數年增；通用型伺服器 2027 年出
貨量與營收亦將維持雙位數增長。預估 2027 年伺服器佔總營收比
重將進一步攀升至 85%~90%，其中 AI 伺服器佔比將突破 80%。
而筆電則受記憶體價格居高不下影響，預估 2027 年出貨量將年減
高個位數至 10%，營收佔比將降至個位數百分比。資本支出方面，
今年前三季資本支出為 346 億元，公司先前宣布終止友達華亞廠
收購案，將轉向擴大海外佈局，預估 2027 年資本支出將略增至
400~450 億元，主要投入美國加州與泰國廠區，以支應未來 12~24
個月 CSP 客戶明確的產能預留需求。預估公司 2027 全年營收 5.85
兆，YoY +42% ，毛利率 4.37%，營利率 2.79%，稅後淨利 1278
億，全年稅後 EPS 31.86 元。

四. 投資建議為「買進」，目標價 385 元 
受惠於 AI 伺服器的強勁需求，公司預估明年 AI 伺服器營收將雙
位數成長，且隨美系四大 CSP 資本支出展望維持正向，GPU、ASIC
伺服器專案持續放量，將有望挹注公司營運持續增長。以公司 2027
年 EPS 來看，目前股價交易於 9~10 倍本益比，評價仍具上修空
間，給予買進投資建議，目標價為 385 元(12x 2027 EPS)。

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介 
廣達電腦(Quanta)為全球筆記型電腦專業研發設計製造與雲端運
算解決方案領導供應商，為全球前五大筆記型電腦代工廠商。公
司主要替品牌業者從事代工服務，代工項目包含筆記型電腦、平
板電腦、智慧型手機、伺服器、商用及家用智慧物聯網裝置、消
費性電子產品、車用電子等。公司總部位於桃園龜山，生產基地
包含桃園、大陸上海、重慶、江蘇、美國田納西州、加州、墨西

圖一、廣達製造基地及全球據點

資料來源：廣達

二.產業趨勢概況 
1. 明年 CSP Capex 預估破 1.5 兆美元，AI 伺服器產值年增逾 9 成 
根據集邦科技(TrendForce)最新研究，受惠於 Google、Amazon、
Meta、Microsoft、Oracle 及中國四大科技巨頭等全球前九大雲端
服務供應商(CSP)擴大投資 AI 基礎設施，預估 2026 年九大 CSP
資本支出總額將逾 9,570 億美元，YoY +106%，2027 年更因伺服
器整機漲價、零組件缺貨漲價及供應商要求預付款等因素，有望
突破 1.5 兆美元，YoY +60%，雙雙優於預期。在超大型 CSP、Tier-2
資料中心與主權 AI 等需求帶動下，預估 2026 年 AI 伺服器產值約
4,790 億美元，占整體伺服器產值近 75%；2027 年隨輝達 GB300、
VR200 與超微 Helios 等整櫃式方案成為主流，加上記憶體等關鍵
零組件短缺推升單價，預計整體伺服器產值將首度跨越 1 兆美元，
其中 AI 伺服器產值將躍升至近 9,200 億美元 YoY +90%，占比進
一步提高至 81%左右。

圖二、2024-2027 年九大 CSP 資本支出總額預估(單位:十億美元)

資料來源：TrendForce、宏遠投顧整理

2. 2026 年全球 AI 伺服器出貨量預計年增 31% 
根據 TrendForce 最新 AI server 產業研究，近期超大型 CSP、
Tier-2 資料中心業者對 NVIDIA GB/VR 等整櫃式 AI server 方案的
採購意願明顯提高，且 Google、AWS 新一代 ASIC 平台於 2026
下半年陸續放量，加上中系業者擴大採用本地 AI 方案以滿足雲端
大型語言模型(LLM) AI 需求，促使 TrendForce 上調 2026 年 AI 
server 出貨年成長幅度，從原先的 28%更新為近 31%。北美五大
業者 2026 年的資本支出多翻倍提升，反映出大型 CSP 廠持續擴
建 AI 資料中心、GPU 叢集、液冷散熱等新一代基礎設施，以應對
生成式 AI 及大型模型快速成長帶來的算力需求。其中，Google 
2026 至 2027 年都將側重自家 TPU 晶片發展，2027 年出貨量有
望繼續高速成長。AWS 2026 年以 NVIDIA GB300 作為 GPU AI 
server 主力，自研 ASIC 也穩定出貨，預期至 2027 年量能將再擴
大。Meta 2026 年主要採用 NVIDIA GB/VR、AMD Helios 整櫃式
方案，自研 ASIC 布局預計至 2027 年將明顯加速，為該公司長期
降低 AI 基礎設施成本、提升推論效率的重要策略。

叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估

一. 4Q26 AI 伺服器動能延續，筆電持續面臨逆風 
公司公告 10 月營收 3532.5 億，MoM +8.6%，YoY +104%，今年
累計營收 3.31 兆，YoY +99.9%。展望 4Q26，公司表示，隨著新
世代 GPU 平台 AI 機櫃於第四季逐步進入小批量量產，受惠於新
產品 ASP 顯著拉升，第四季 AI 伺服器營收仍將維持強勁季增。
通用型伺服器第四季出貨量亦預計持續成長，而筆電第四季出貨
量則預期呈雙位數季減。另外，毛利率部分，由於高單價 AI 機櫃
出貨佔比提升持續稀釋毛利率，再加上筆電出貨規模縮減，預期

## Document 112: 261108_ms_novatek

### Page 1

M
Update
Novatek  | Asia Pacific
Consumer Demand Softens as
Cost Pressure Builds
3Q growth led by smartphones: Revenue was NT$30.97bn, +8% Q/Q and +26% Y/
Y, 7% above MSe. Net income was NT$6.08bn, -4% Q/Q and +66% Y/Y, 2% above
MSe, with EPS of NT$9.99. Smartphone restocking lifted sales, but GM fell 1.53ppt
Q/Q to 40.89% on a lower SoC mix and a lag in cost pass-through. Lower opex
supported a 9.6% Q/Q rise in operating profit; net income declined in the absence of

2Q's NT$860mn one-off investment gain.
4Q revenue and margins seen declining sequentially: Management guided for
revenue of NT$28.0-29.2bn, down 6-10% Q/Q, with GM of 37-39% and OpM of 17-
19%, assuming NT$/US$32. Large DDICs should face the largest sequential revenue
drop, particularly from notebooks, followed by SoC; small/medium DDICs should
decrease slightly despite stronger Chinese OLED handset orders. Lower-cost
inventory depletion, product mix and higher memory KGD costs will likely weigh on

margins. Prices have been adjusted across SoC and DDIC products, but management
still sees higher memory prices constraining consumer demand into 2027.
New products offer growth, with cost risks unresolved: Automotive eDP TDDI is
scheduled for mass production in 4Q26, while touch-enabled notebook products are
scheduled to ramp through 2027. OLED solutions for IT applications and higher-
performance handset products broaden opportunities, although smartphone OLED

DDIC shipments are expected to decline Y/Y in 2026. Management expects new
products to support 2027 revenue growth but provided no quantified target or
assurance of flat Y/Y margins. Further foundry and OSAT price increases remain
under negotiation, leaving earnings dependent upon product mix and the pace of
cost pass-through.
Morgan Stanley Taiwan Limited+
Daniel Yen, CFA
Equity Analyst

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Novatek (3034.TW)
Base case, residual income model. Key assumptions: cost of equity constant at 9.8% (2.0%
risk-free rate, 6% risk premium, 1.3 beta), payout ratio of 80%, medium-term growth rate of
6.1% and terminal growth rate assumption of 3.4%.
Risks to Upside
n Faster-than-expected 8K/4K TV growth.
n Better-than-expected smartphone sell-through.
n Better-than-expected sell-through of foldable products.

Risks to Downside
n Intensified price competition in TDDI, driving Novatek's gross margin below its corpo-
rate average.
n Slower-than-expected 8K/4K TV growth.
n Slower sell-through of foldable products.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views

below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

## Document 173: 261112_ml_kingslide

Source PDF: all report pdf/261112_ml_kingslide.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

King Slide

Buy: strong 3Q26 results, 88.2% GM; solid 
’27/28E sales/margin; cut PO to NT$12,988

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 12,988 TWD | Price: 8,165 TWD

3Q26 results: another impressive beat with 88.2% GM 
King Slide’s 3Q26 GM/OPM hit record highs of 88.2%/82.9% for the fourth/second 
consecutive quarter, respectively, well above BofAe of 83.7%/78.2% and consensus of 
84.0%/78.6%. OP (+62% QoQ, +383% YoY) was 7%/5% above BofAe/consensus. We 
attribute the solid margins/results to sizable sales scale (+60% QoQ, +297% YoY) and 
better operating efficiency. With non-operating gain (mainly net interest income), NP 
(+65% QoQ, +266% YoY) was 8%/7% above BofAe/consensus (Exhibit 3). 
BofAe 4Q26 sales -20% QoQ; solid 2027/28 sales/margin 
We now expect King Slide’s 4Q26 sales to decline 20% QoQ, from the high 3Q26 base 
and amid GPU platform transition. We believe Sep/Oct sales (-31%/+2% MoM) shall 
serve as evidence for slower order momentum. Our 4Q26 sales forecast implies flat-to-
slightly higher MoM sales in Nov/Dec. We now expect another 45%/20% YoY sales 
growth in 2027/28, with stronger pull-in demand resuming in 1Q27. In our view, 
2027/28 sales should be supported by various AI projects across platforms (GPU/ASIC/ 
AMD) and clients (global/regional CSPs, neoclouds, AI start-ups, enterprises). We also 
keep monitoring the capacity ramp-up from the new US Houston site, which may bring 
further sales upside. We expect high and sustainable GM at 85.0% both in 2027/28. We 
see FX as a potential swing factor to GM, given the company’s natural-hedging policy.  
Trim PO to NT$12,988: lower P/E set-up; reiterate Buy 
We lower our 2026-27E EPS by 0-4% but lift our 2028E EPS by 7%, mainly to reflect 
3Q26 beat and stronger margins. Yet, we cut PO to NT$12,988 (28x 2027E EPS) from 
NT$14,888 (32x 2027E EPS), with valuation base unchanged but a lower P/E multiple. 
We now use 28x (2SD above average since IPO) on valuation de-rating across AI supply 
chain amid macro uncertainty. Nevertheless, we reiterate Buy on King Slide in view of its 
lion share in AI server rail market, diversified client/project base, strong margin profile.

Company Sector 
IT Hardware

Company Description 
King Slide is the global top server rail kit manufacturer from 
Taiwan. It also supplies slide rail kit for kitchen/furniture. Its 
key server clients include global cloud service providers and 
enterprise servers.

King Slide was founded in 1986 and was publicly listed on 
Taiwan Stock Exchange on 25 June 2008. Its headquarter 
and main production sites are located in Kaohsiung 
(Taiwan). It also has capacity in Houston (US).

Investment Rationale 
We assign a Buy rating to King Slide given its leading 
position in server rail to ride on strong AI market demand 
with rising content value thanks to increasing component 
density and customization. We highlight King Slide's early 
step into server market since 2001 with proven track 
record, much larger scale, time-to-market capability, higher 
automation level, and 3,600+ patents as strong moats for 
high/above-peer margins.

### Page 6

Price objective basis & risk 
King Slide (KGSLF) 
Our PO of NT$12,988 is based on 28x 2027E EPS. We use 28x P/E multiple (2SD above 
P/E average since listing) in view of strong AI server market demand, leading position in 
server rail, solid/high margin profiles, and double-digit sales/EPS CAGR in 2026-28E. Its 
early step into server market since 2001 with proven track record, much larger scale 
than peer, and 3,600+ patents as strong moats for high/above-peer margins.

Upside risks to our PO are: (1) even stronger AI server demand, (2) margin upside thanks 
to larger scale and/or better efficiency, (3) better kitchen slides market demand, (4) 
faster capacity expansion, (5) favorable FX movement (mainly USD/NTD) for GM and/or 
non-operating items.

Downside risks to our PO are: (1) softer AI server demand due to GPU/components 
shortage, (2) GM pressure due to higher raw material cost (e.g. steel, aluminum), (3) 
softer kitchen slides market demand under macro uncertainty/weakness, (4) delayed 
product certification, (5) slower capacity expansion, (6) unfavorable FX movement 
(mainly USD/NTD) to weigh on GM and/or non-operating items.

### Page 1

M
Update
Compal Electronics  | Asia Pacific
Server Ramp Takes Shape but
Valuation Keeps Us UW
Compal’s server expansion is progressing, with revenue
contribution rising to ~12% in 3Q and management targeting 30-
40% in CY27. However, weaker PC volumes and diminishing
scale benefits continue to pressure margins. At ~14x CY27E P/E,
valuation remains stretched.
3Q OpM missed. See our  3Q26 Results Summary                   .
PC business facing demand and profitability headwinds: Compal expects a mid-

teens q/q decline in 4Q26 shipments because of higher selling prices. This weakness
should persist into CY27; the company projects a likely double-digit y/y unit decline,
though it admitted that visibility remains low. However, rising ASPs from mix
changes and cost pass-through could keep 4Q PC revenue up sequentially.
Servers have become a key growth driver: Servers rose from ~5% of the mix in 1Q
to ~12% in 3Q, with a CY27 revenue contribution target of 30-40%. While 4Q26

server revenue is guided to be flat q/q, Compal is aggressively scaling its capacity. It
now primarily ships from Taiwan, and a major L10/11 ramp will begin in 2Q27 at its
new Texas plant. It currently does L10 HGX for Neocloud customers and L6 for a US
enterprise customer; it will do L10/11 for enterprise customer(s) as well, and is
actively approaching CSP customers for ASIC L11 solutions as well.
Margins to remain pressured: Overall margins were pressured by the PC segment,

driving a sequential decline in 3Q owing to lower PC volumes and utilization. This
margin headwind should intensify in 4Q because of shrinking PC business scale.
Conversely, server gross margin should remain resilient in the high single digits to
10%,  staying flat q/q.
4Q preview: We estimate that 4Q revenue will grow ~8% q/q, to NT$263bn (+38%
y/y), primarily driven by higher PC ASPs despite declining volumes. GM should
contracting ~10bps q/q, to 4.3% (-150bps y/y), with declining PC profitability, mainly

owing to diminishing scale benefits. This leads to our OP estimate of NT$2.8bn
(+25% q/q, +6% y/y) and NP estimate of NT$1.7bn (-34% q/q, +21% y/y), for EPS of
NT$0.39. Our 4Q net profit estimate is down q/q because of lower non-op income.
Stay UW with price target unchanged at NT$23.0 (10.4x CY27 P/E). We think
valuation looks quite stretched now at ~14x CY27e P/E. We prefer other ODMs like
Wistron (6x), Hon Hai (12x), Wiwynn (8x), or Quanta (9x).

Morgan Stanley appreciates your support in
the 2027 Extel Asia Research Survey.

3Q26 gross margin miss:

+33% y/y), 15% above our estimate/2% above the Street, for EPS of NT$0.59.

Price Target and Valuation Methodology
Our 12-month price target is unchanged at  NT$23.0: Our price target is our base case
scenario value, derived from a multistage residual income (RI) model. We use the
company's beginning equity plus the present value of all expected residual income in
excess of the cost of capital. The RI is positive when ROAE is above the cost of capital and
negative when it is below.
We assume a cost of equity of 9.1%  (beta of 1.3, equity risk premium of 6.0% and risk-free

rate of 1.5%), a medium-term growth rate of 5%, and a terminal growth rate of 3% (all
unchanged).
Our new price target implies a 2026e P/E of 10.4x and a 2027e P/E of 8.5x, below the five-
year average of 13.8x. We view these as fair, because we factor in more conservative
assumptions for its consumer business and a weaker margin outlook for Compal. The
company's venture into the "new businesses" (e.g., auto, server, etc.) is constraining its
margin profile as it tries to shift from 80%+ PC to more commercial exposure.

Our bull and bear case values are also unchanged, at NT$38.0 and NT$17.0,
respectively.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Hon Hai Precision (2317.TW)
Base case scenario value, derived from a residual income valuation model. Our key assump-
tions include a cost of equity of 8.5%, a medium-term growth rate of 13%, and a terminal
growth rate of 3%.
Risks to Upside
n Better-than-expected iPhone sell-through
n Faster progress in AI server business
n Faster EV business development progress
n Any new M&A activity that could improve sentiment

Risks to Downside
n Lower iPhone sell-through
n Smaller profit contribution from AI business
n Slower EV business development progress
n Geopolitical developments that could negatively affect foreign investment
Quanta Computer Inc. (2382.TW)
Base case, residual income model. Key assumptions include a cost of equity of 9.0% (beta of
1.2, equity premium of 6.0% and risk-free rate of 1.5%), a 7.0% medium-term growth rate, and
a 3% terminal growth rate.

Risks to Upside
n Stronger-than-expected NB demand
n Stronger-than-expected Apple Watch demand
n Stronger-than-expected server demand
n Faster-than-expected AI server penetration
Risks to Downside
n Weaker-than-expected NB demand
n Softer-than-expected Apple Watch demand
n Weak margin performance owing to rising labor costs and sales shortfalls
n Fierce price competition in the mega data center segment
n Slower-than-expected AI server penetration

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

## Document 190: 4连涨！短期美债收益率创2022年以来最快涨幅，市场押注美联储加息

Source PDF: all report pdf/4连涨！短期美债收益率创2022年以来最快涨幅，市场押注美联储加息.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-13
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

美国短期国债收益率连续四个交易⽇⼤幅攀升， 2 年期收益率升⾄ 4.862% ，累计涨幅 38.8
个基点，创 2022 年 6 ⽉以来最⼤四⽇涨幅。 10 年期收益率同步突破 5.10% 关键阈值。实际
利率同步攀升，市场已将终端利率预期⼤幅上调⾄ 4.82% ， 12 ⽉加息基本已成定局， 1 ⽉再
度加息的概率则⾼达 75% 。
摘要：
美国短期国债收益率在四个交易⽇内完成了⾃ 2022 年加息周期以来最⼤幅度的跳升，市场正在
重新定价美联储政策预期 —— 押注利率将更⾼、更持久。
周四，对美联储政策最为敏感的 2 年期美债收益率升⾄ 4.862% ，过去四个交易⽇（不计周三退
伍军⼈节休市）累计上⾏ 38.8 个基点，创下⾃ 2022 年 6 ⽉ 15 ⽇以来最⼤四交易⽇涨幅。
与此同时， 10 年期美债收益率也突破 5.10% ，刷新⼼理关键阈值，对美股构成压⼒。
此轮抛售的导⽕索接连引爆： 10 ⽉美国 CPI 数据⾼于预期、劳动⼒市场依然⽕热，美联储理事沃
勒周⼆暗⽰ 12 ⽉或需加息 50 个基点，克利夫兰联储主席 Hammack 重申政策须转向限制性⽴场，

加之地缘政治升温推⾼油价，三重压⼒叠加，加速了国债市场的抛售。
TD Securities 美国利率策略主管 Gennadiy Goldberg 表⽰：
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

通胀黏性与鹰派官员合⼒施压
周四引发市场震荡的，是多重因素的密集共振。
其⼀， 10 ⽉ CPI 数据的强劲令市场担忧通胀依然⼴泛且具黏性，进⽽质疑美联储当前渐进式加息
节奏是否⾜以应对通胀压⼒。
其⼆，多位美联储官员持续释放鹰派信号，克利夫兰联储主席 Hammack 上⽉曾投票⽀持加息，
当天重申货币政策必须转⼊限制性区间，⽅能将通胀压回 2% ⽬标。
其三，地缘政治局势的新进展推升油价，进⼀步强化了通胀⻛险叙事，加速了国债抛售。
三重因素叠加，当⽇美债收益率单⽇上⾏ 12 ⾄ 13 个基点，其中⻓端表现相对抗跌，但 10 年期收
益率仍上⾏约 8 个基点；⼀场疲弱的 30 年期国债标售结果更是雪上加霜，令⻓端收益率进⼀步承
压， 10 年期收益率最终升破 5.10% 。
实际利率同步攀升，加息预期⼤幅上移
此轮债市重定价幅度显著。
TD Securities 美国利率策略主管 Gennadiy Goldberg 认为，交易员周四已将 " 终端利率 " 预期从数
⽇前的 4.44% ⼤幅上调⾄ 4.82% ，并开始押注美联储在明年 1 ⽉前将累计再加息两次 —— 其中 12

⽉加息基本已成定局， 1 ⽉再度加息的概率则⾼达 75% 。
此次收益率急升本质上是市场对美联储预期重新定价的结果，反映出未来数⽉更为鹰派的政策⾛
向。

### Page 3

美联储今年 9 ⽉时隔三年⾸度重启加息，将联邦基⾦利率⽬标区间上调⾄ 3.75% ⾄ 4% 。
此前⼀轮⾃ 2022 年初启动、从接近零利率出发的激进加息周期，曾将利率上限推⾄ 2023 ⾄ 2024
年间的 5.5% 峰值，彼时的加息节奏为 1987 年以来最快。
值得注意的是，此轮收益率上⾏并⾮单纯由通胀预期驱动。10 年期通胀保值债券（ TIPS ）实际
收益率周四同样⼤幅跳升，连续第五个交易⽇上⾏，年初⾄今累计涨幅达 90 个基点。
分析认为，实际收益率的同步攀升意味着，市场的抛售逻辑不仅仅是对通胀的担忧，更折射出
投资者对美联储将实施真实货币紧缩的坚定预期。这对⻛险资产的压⼒，或将较单纯的通胀预
期升温更为持久。
沃什⻛格下的⾼波动时代
此轮债市动荡，也是美联储主席沃什上任以来市场⽣态转变的缩影。
沃什于今年 5 ⽉接任美联储主席⼀职，主张联储减少前瞻性沟通、更多地跟随市场信号⾏事，其
⻛格与前任形成鲜明对⽐。他还计划明年减少⼀次联邦公开市场委员会议息会议。
这种更为低调、被动的沟通⽅式，已被市场解读为政策不确定性上升的信号，并在⼀定程度上

放⼤了债市对于数据和官员⾔论的价格敏感性。

### Page 4

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

回溯历史， 2022 年那轮加息周期中，⾼利率冲击虽未令美国经济陷⼊衰退，却直接导致硅⾕银
⾏倒闭，并引发了 2023 年那场短暂⽽剧烈的区域性银⾏业危机。
如今，随着收益率再度逼近历史⾼位，市场对利率冲击的潜在连锁效应，正重新提⾼警觉。

## Document 222: [005930] 삼성전자(005930) 실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다삼성전자(005930) 실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다삼성전자(005930) 실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
아직 수요의 끝은 보이지 않는다 
AI 투자에 대한 우려의 목소리도 있지만 정작 부품을 공급하고 투자하는 쪽의 목소
리는 시간이 갈수록 더 커지고 있다. 공급 부족의 시점 매 분기 뒤로 밀려나고 있기 
때문이다. 28년까지 공급부족이라고 했던 메모리업체들은 이구동성으로 30년까지 공
급부족이 지속된다고 언급하고 있다. 이러한 변화는 실적에 고스란히 반영되고 있
다. 26년 3분기 메모리 영업이익만 108.5조원으로 추정한다. 4분기에는 140조원에 
이를 것으로 전망한다. 
HBM4와 QLC는 차별화 포인트 
삼성전자는 HBM에서 입지가 좋았던 적이 없었다. 점유율, 수익성, 가동률, 제품 성
능, 고객 등 모든 항목에서 경쟁사 대비 열위에 있었다. HBM4는 모든 항목에서 입
지를 개선시킬 변수가 될 것으로 예상한다. 26년 3분기 물량은 경쟁사 수준일 것으
로 보이고 연간 물량도 유사한 수준일 전망이다. 27년에는 가격 인상과 가동률 상승
으로 수익성도 크게 개선될 것으로 기대한다. QLC는 TLC와 함께 AI 모델 진화의 
수혜를 받을 제품으로 빠르게 부상하고 있다. 메타의 Muse 영향이다. 접속자당 가
상 메모리를 NAND에 100GB를 할당하고 있기 때문이다.  
부족한 주주환원으로 투자 우선 순위에서 밀릴 듯 
삼성전자에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. 이는 여러 변수에도 메모리 성장은 
변화가 없을 것으로 전망하고, 메모리 수요는 장기적 성장 국면에 진입했고, HBM 
시장 내 입지가 강화되고 있고, 현재 주가는 실적 대비 지나치게 저평가된 수준으로 
판단하기 때문이다. 목표주가는 350,000원으로 하향한다. 현재 수익성과 시장 상황
을 고려하면 적정 주가는 460,000원으로 판단하지만 주주환원 정책이 시장 기대치
에 미치지 못하고, 경쟁사와 비교해도 선호도가 낮아질 것을 고려하면 주가 상승은 
제한적일 것으로 판단하기 때문이다.

### Page 2

삼성전자 (005930)

2 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
26년 3분기 매출액은 195.3조원 
삼성전자의 26년 3분기 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 13.9% 증가한 195.3조원이다. VD/
가전을 제외한 주요 사업부 매출액은 2분기 대비 증가했다. DS, MX 모두 물량보다는 
가격 효과가 더 크게 작용한 것으로 분석된다. 사업부별로는  
1) DS 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 15.4% 증가한 147.1조원이다. 메모리
는 26년 2분기 대비 16.4% 증가했다. 26년 3분기 DRAM B/G(Bit Growth)
는 +2.0%, ASP는 +20.0%이다. NAND B/G는 +5.0%, ASP는 +18.0%이다. 
HBM 물량 증가는 HBM4가 주도했고, 그 이하 제품 비중은 하락한 것으로 
추정한다.  
2) 디스플레이 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 7.1% 증가한 8조원이다. 소형
은 해외 고객 및 국내 고객의 신제품 출시 영향으로 ASP 상승, 물량 증가 
효과가 동반되었고, 대형은 게이밍 모니터 비중을 확대했다. 
3) MX/네트워크 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 13.4% 증가한 37.7조원이
다. MX는 15.5% 증가했다. 물량은 소폭 감소했고, ASP 상승 영향을 받았다. 
네트워크 사업부 매출액은 2분기 대비 60% 감소했다.  
4) VD/가전 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 4.1% 감소한 13.9조원이다. VD 
매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 6.5% 감소했다. 가전은 26년 2분기 대비 1.3% 
감소했다.

26년 3분기 영업이익은 107.4조원 
삼성전자의 26년 3분기 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 20% 증가한 107.4조원이다. DS,  
디스플레이와 MX는 2분기 대비 증가했고, VD/가전은 2분기 대비 감소했다. 사업부별
로는 
1) DS 사업부는 26년 2분기 대비 19.4% 증가한 106.5조원이다. 2분기 대비 영
업이익률은 소폭 상승했다. 메모리 영업이익은 2분기 대비 19.0% 증가했고, 
비메모리는 영업적자를 유지했다.  
2) 디스플레이 사업부 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 54.5% 증가한 1.1조원이다. 
중소형 신제품 효과로 수익성이 개선된 영향이다. 대형도 믹스 개선으로 수
익성이 개선되었다. 
3) MX/네트워크 사업부 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 적자폭이 감소했다. 물량
은 줄었지만 High End 비중 상승으로 ASP는 개선되었고, 원가 비중이 낮아
진 영향이다. 
4) VD/가전 사업부 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 적자 규모가 커졌다.

### Page 6

삼성전자 (005930)

6 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
26년 4분기 매출액은 226.5조원으로 예상 
삼성전자의 26년 4분기 매출액은 26년 3분기 대비 16.0% 증가한 226.5조원으로 예상
한다. DS가 증가하고 나머지는 부진할 것으로 예상한다.  
DS는 메모리가 성장을 주도할 것으로 기대한다. 가격과 물량 모두 3분기 대비 증가할 
것으로 전망한다. Context Server에 대한 수요가 증가하고, Personal Agent의 가상메
모리 설정으로 인해 NAND 수요가 증가하면서 QLC에 대한 수요 증가 속도가 빨라질 
전망이다. 이로 인해 NAND Bit Growth는 경쟁사와 차별화된 수준일 것으로 예상한
다.  
HBM 물량도 꾸준히 증가할 것으로 기대한다. 3분기에도 HBM4를 중심으로 물량이 
크게 증가했다. HBM4는 SK하이닉스와 비슷한 물량일 것으로 예상한다. 아직 전체 
물량은 SK하이닉스가 더 많은 상황이고, HBM 내 HBM4 비중은 업계 최고 수준인 것
으로 판단한다. 26년까지는 HBM이 수익성에는 마이너스 요인이지만, 27년에는 
DRAM 수준의 영업이익이 가능할 것으로 기대한다.  
MX는 비수기 저가 라인 물량 조정으로 전체 물량은 감소하지만, 제품믹스는 개선되
어 판가는 3분기 대비 상승할 것으로 예상한다.

26년 4분기 영업이익은 138.2조원으로 예상 
삼성전자의 26년 4분기 영업이익은 26년 3분기 대비 28.7% 증가한 138.2조원으로 예
상한다. DS 사업부를 제외하고 모두 3분기 대비 부진할 것으로 예상한다.  
DS 사업부 영업이익은 3분기 대비 30% 증가한 138.5조원으로 전망한다. DRAM, 
NAND 가격이 10% 초중반 수준으로 상승하고, 물량은 10% 중반 이상 증가하면서 3
분기 대비 영업이익률 개선을 기대하기 때문이다. HBM 물량 증가가 이익 개선에 미
치는 영향은 제한적일 것으로 예상한다.  
디스플레이, MX는 물량 부진으로 3분기 대비 영업이익은 부진할 것으로 전망한다.

DS가 성장 주도 
DS만 보자

### Page 7

김운호  6915-5656

IBKS RESEARCH │7 
Check Point 
1. 모자란 듯한 주주환원 
26년 3분기 실적 발표에서 밝힌 주주환원 정책은 지난 8월의 내용과 다르지 않다. 3
분기 배당 30조원(주당 4,601원)을 확정한 정도이다. 잔여 배당 재원이 55~75조원으
로 구체화했고, 현금 배당과 자사주 매입을 병행하겠다고 밝혔다.  
자사주 매입 시 우선주 매입도 고려 대상이라고 언급한 것은 고무적이지만 27년부터 
새롭게 시작되는 주주환원 정책을 결정하기 위한 이사회 개최는 27년 1월에 열릴 것
으로 예고하였기에 연내 추가적인 주주환원 발표는 없을 것으로 판단한다.  
2. HBM4로 HBM 시장에서 입지 강화 
HBM4를 통해서 자신감을 회복하고 있는 것으로 판단한다. 양산 시점은 경쟁사 대비 
빨랐고, 3분기 물량은 경쟁사와 비슷한 수준인 것으로 추정한다. HBM3e가 부진했기 
때문에 전체 생산량에서 HBM에서 HBM4 비중은 가장 높은 수준일 것으로 추정한다.  
26년 전체 물량도 경쟁사와 비슷한 수준이 될 것으로 전망한다. 삼성전자는 HBM 시
장 점유율을 DRAM 수준으로 맞추겠다는 목표를 3분기에 달성한 것으로 밝혔다.  
물량이 증가하면서 가동률도 빠르게 개선될 것으로 기대한다. 아직은 DRAM과 영업
이익률에서 큰 차이가 있지만 27년에는 인상된 가격, 높아진 수율, 가동률 개선으로 
DRAM과 영업이익률 차이를 좁힐 수 있을 것으로 기대한다.  
3. QLC NAND 시장 확대 
AI 컴퓨팅 확산은 NAND에게도 성장 기회이다. AI 모델들의 Context Window가 길어
져 KV Cache 메모리에 대한 수요가 커지면서 High Endurance TLC 수요가 급증하고 
있다. DRAM을 대체할 수준의 NAND가 필요하게 되었기 때문이다.  
이 뿐만 아니라 고용량 NAND에 대한 수요도 Personal Agent의 출현으로 급속히 증
가할 것으로 기대한다. 여기에는 QLC NAND가 주로 사용된다. 메타의 Muse는 접속
자 1인에 대해서 DRAM 8GB, NAND 100GB의 가상 메모리를 설정하고 운영한다.  
Agent AI의 확산은 스토리지에 대한 수요도 급격히 확대할 것으로 기대하고, 삼성전
자에게도 기회가 될 것으로 기대한다.  
26년 4분기 NAND Bit Growth는 삼성전자가 가장 높을 것으로 예상한다.

### Page 8

삼성전자 (005930)

8 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 350,000원으로 하향 
삼성전자에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. 이는 
1) 여러 변수에도 메모리 성장은 변화가 없을 것으로 전망하고, 
2) 메모리 수요는 장기적 성장 국면에 진입했고, 
3) HBM 시장 내 입지가 강화되고 있고, 
4) 현재 주가는 실적 대비 지나치게 저평가된 수준으로 판단하기 때문이다. 
목표주가는 350,000원으로 하향한다. 현재 수익성과 시장 상황을 고려하면 적정 주가
는 460,000원으로 판단하지만 주주환원 정책이 시장 기대치에 미치지 못하고, 경쟁사
와 비교해도 선호도가 낮아질 것을 고려하면 주가 상승은 제한적일 것으로 판단하기 
때문이다.

## Document 233: [034020] 두산에너빌리티(034020) 시장과 산업의 시차. 2026 SMR 첫걸음. 2H27 원전 수 주 확대 본격화두산에너빌리티(034020) 시장과 산업의 시차_47af979a

Source PDF: all report pdf/[034020] 두산에너빌리티(034020) 시장과 산업의 시차. 2026 SMR 첫걸음. 2H27 원전 수 주 확대 본격화두산에너빌리티(034020) 시장과 산업의 시차_47af979a.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-30

### Page 1

기업분석 2026.10.30 www.daishin.com

두산 
에너빌리티 
(034020)

시장과 산업의 시차. 2026 
SMR 첫걸음. 2H27 원전 수
주 확대 본격화 
허민호

minho.hur@daishin.com

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 
목표주가 85,000 
하향  
현재주가 
(26.10.29) 
56,700 
유틸리티업종

◼ 목표주가 85,000원(하향). 3분기 실적, 소규모 SMR 수주 등은 긍정적 
◼ 구체적인 미국 원전 사업개발 시작, 2H27 장납기품목 선발주 기대 
◼ 2027년 체코 원전 시공 입찰 재시도, 시공능력의 중요성 강조

목표주가 85,000원(하향). 3분기 실적 양호, 소규모 SMR 수주는 긍정적 
투자의견 ‘매수’ 유지, 목표주가 85,000원으로 하향. 이제는 성장 스토리보다
는 실질적인 원전 수주 확대가 필요한 상황. 의미 있는 수준의 원전 수주 확
대는 2H27부터 본격화될 가능성 존재. 다만, 단기적인 원전 수주 모멘텀 부
족 등을 감안해 6개월 목표주가를 현실화했음 
1) 3Q26 연결기준 영업이익 2,612억원(+91%, 이하 YoY), 에너빌리티 부문 
영업이익 1,211억원(+179%) 등 양호한 실적 기록. 이는 에너빌리티의 가스
터빈, 원전 기자재 매출 증가 등에 기인, 2) 10월 29일 NuScale과 1개 모듈 
주기기 공급 계약을 체결했으며, 4Q26 X-energy와 4개 모듈의 일부 장납기 
품목/소재 수주가 예상되는 등 2Q26 ~ 3Q26 TerraPower와의 일부 품목 계
약에 이어 개별 건의 규모는 작지만 다수의 SMR 관련 기자재 수주가 늘어나
는 점은 긍정적. 3) 최근 폴란드 AP1000 3기의 선발주 장납기 품목 확정(폴
란드 공급업체의 대상 생산 품목 확정, 원자로 압력용기 및 증기발생기는 미
확정)되었으며, 미국 원전 프로젝트가 구체화되기 시작. 4) 향후 AP1000향 
공급 기자재 품목 확대 가능성도 존재. 이는 2H27 본격적인 원전 수주금액 
확대로 이어질 것으로 기대

미국 원전은 구체적 사업개발 시작, 2H27 이후 장납기품목 선발주 기대 
2026년 6월 DOE의 ‘원전 공급망 금융지원 사업 발표(AP1000 10기)’, 8월 
TVA의 ‘2026 IRP’에 2040년까지 최대 원전 5.4GW 승인, 9월 한국과 미국 
상무부 간의 원전 투자 8기 MOU 체결 등을 통해 미국 원전 건설 재개 정책 
및 제도 개편이 구체화되고 강화되었음. 미국 원전 시장은 4Q26 ~ 2027년 
구체적인 사업개발 단계를 거쳐(부지 선정, 원전 운영 SPC 설립, 금융계약체
결, COL 신청 등), 2H27 ~ 2028년 장납기품목 선발주, 2028 ~ 2029년 건
설 계약 단계 진입 예상. 동사는 4Q26 미국 및 국내 가스터빈, 국내 공공 해
상풍력 프로젝트용 터빈, 폴란드 AP1000 주기기 등 미국 원전 이외 분야에
서의 수주 확대 예상. 현재 Rolls-Royce 등과도 SMR 주기기 공급 관련 논의 
중이며, NuScale SMR은 2027년 PPA 체결 시, COL 및 FID 이전 SMR 모듈 
11기(4Q26 1기 계약)의 수주가 가능할 것으로 기대

2027년 체코 원전 시공 입찰 재시도, 시공능력의 중요성 강조 
대우건설·두산에너빌리티 컨소시엄의 체코 원전 시공 계약 협상은 종료되었
지만, 2027년 7월 신규 시공사와 계약 예정. 동사는 입찰에 다시 참여할 예
정. 이번 이슈의 핵심 쟁점은 이미 체코 측과 공사비총액(Lump-sum) 계약을 
맺은 한수원이 시공사와 총액계약을 맺기를 원한 것임. 이번 이슈로 체코 두
코바니 원전 EPC 프로젝트 수익성에 대한 우려가 발생할 수 있지만, 실제 건
설 과정에서 추가 비용이 공사비 계약금액(예비비 포함)을 초과할지는 예단하
기 어려움. 하도급 관리, 공기 준수 등 시공능력의 중요성이 강조되는 상황. 
동사는 3년 전부터 체코에 시공 인력 상주하며 시공 환경 자료 확보, 초기 인
허가, 설계 업무 관여해 왔으며, 주기기 공급사로서 기자재 특성을 활용해 설
치까지 수행 가능한 경쟁력 등을 바탕으로 성공적 수주를 목표하고 있음

표 4. DOE의 미국 대형원전 공급망 금융지원 사업의 대상 장납기품목(LLI; Long-Lead Items) 
 품목 설명  대출과 관련된 확인 근거 
원자로압력용기 
(RPV; Reactor Pressure Vessel) 
핵연료와 노심을 수용하고 원자로 냉각재의 압력을 유지하는 
용기. 앞서 설명한 격납용기와 구분되는 원자로 주기기 DOE 공식 설명자료 — 2026.06.23 
증기발생기 
(SG; Steam Generator) 
원자로에서 나온 열을 전달받아 터빈을 돌릴 증기를 만드는 대
형 열교환기 DOE 공식 설명자료 — 2026.06.23 
사전제작 구조 모듈 
(PSM; Prefabricated Structural Modules) 
원전 건물의 벽체·바닥 등 구조물을 모듈로 미리 제작하는 방
식의 구성품. AP1000에는 강판과 콘크리트로 구성하는 구조 
모듈이 사용됨 
DOE 공식 설명자료 — 2026.06.23. 다만, 미국 대
출자료에 개별 모듈 코드까지 열거되지는 않음 
원자로냉각재펌프 
(RCP; Reactor Coolant Pump) 
원자로 냉각재를 순환시켜 원자로에서 발생한 열을 증기발생
기로 전달하는 펌프 DOE 공식 설명자료 — 2026.06.23 
격납용기 
(CV; Containment Vessel) 
원자로압력용기와 주요 설비를 둘러싸며 방사성 물질의 외부 
방출을 억제하는 대형 강철 밀폐 구조물 
DOE EDF 담당자의 직접 발언에서 확인 — 
2026.10.20 공개 인터뷰 
자료: DOE, 대신증권 Research Center

주3: 원자력, 가스/수소/양수발전 기자재/서비스 매출액 및 매출비중은 대신증권 추정치

ASIA MONTHLY REPORT  November 2026, No.308

The Japan Research Institute, Limited  
Economics Department

1 
Topics  Regulatory reform in India's insurance sector

Over the past quarter century, the Indian government has pursued the liberalization of the country's insurance market, 
with the aim of bringing stability to people's living standards and strengthening financial intermediation functions. 
Recently, however, it has begun to reverse course, tightening rules in order to better protect consumers.

■ India's insurance market has grown rapidly with deregulation 
The Indian government has long pursued 
the development of the country's insurance 
market, with the aim of bringing stability to 
people's living standards and strengthening 
financial intermediation functions. This 
stance can be attributed to two background 
factors:  
The first is that India's social security 
system remains underdeveloped. As a result, 
enrollment in health and life insurance can 
provide a vital safety net for mitigating the 
risk of sharp declines in household income 
due to unexpected illnesses or accidents.  
The second is that the sustainable 
development of the Indian economy requires 
the accumulation of long-term and stable 
domestic capital, separate from short-term 
bank deposits, in order to finance the 
development of infrastructure and the 
nurturing of industry. The hope has been that 
the enormous sums of money collected and 
managed by insurance companies can serve 
as a reliable source for this long-term 
financing.  
These factors have led the Indian 
government to walk a path of gradual 
deregulation over the past quarter century. 
The process began with the liberalization of 
market entry by private and foreign 
companies in 2000, which was followed by 
a lifting of the ban on "bancassurance" (the 
sale of insurance through bank branches) in 
2002, the raising of the ceiling on foreign 
ownership, and other reforms.  
This series of deregulatory measures 
spurred the entry of numerous private and foreign 
firms, causing market competition to intensify. Several 
Japanese insurers, including Nippon Life and the Tokio 
Marine Group, also entered the market, helping to drive 
investment in India.  
Besides deregulation related to market entry and 
sales, other factors also contributed to the rapid growth 
of India's insurance market, namely the expansion of 
the middle class, improvements in household financial 
literacy, and the digitalization of insurance services. 
Premium income grew at a pace exceeding economic 
growth, and in recent years insurance companies' assets 
under management have reached a level equivalent to 
Year Event
Establishment of the IRDAI (Insurance Regulatory
and Development Authority of India)
Acceptance of market entry applications from
domestic private firms and foreign entities
(foreign equity cap set at 26%)
2002
Authorization of bancassurance (sale of insurance
through banks or bank-owned distribution
channels)
2015 Increase in foreign equity cap from 26% to 49%
2016
Introduction of a post-facto notification system
for the sale of new insurance products (replacing
the previous prior-approval system)

incentives to sell products and inappropriate sales practices. This should shore up the credibility of the 
insurance market and the foundation for its sustainable development. 
On the minus side, there are worries about side effects arising from the transition. As rules on selling 
become stricter, insurance companies and the financial institutions acting as insurance agents will inevitably 
see some temporary moderation in the pace of their earnings growth. Moreover, given that insurance 
companies and financial institutions are major investors in India's financial markets, tighter regulation is 
likely to exert downward pressure on equity and bond prices. 
These changes in the insurance market provide an important perspective for assessing the future 
trajectory of the Indian economy. They illustrate that rapid market expansion driven by liberalization can 
create a variety of problems. And this dynamic is not limited to the insurance sector, as it can also occur 
across many other industries. At the same time, the redesign and recalibration of regulations in response to 
such developments can provide a foundation for a new stage of growth. The new insurance regulations 
could constitute an early example of this phenomenon, so will be closely watched to see what changes they 
bring in the years ahead.  
  (Shotaro Kumagai)

Topics  Talent travel curbs may weigh on China’s growth

In September, the Chinese government introduced new exit-entry rules. Indicative of an effort to retain talent in order 
to prevent technology leakage, the rules could narrow opportunities for China to absorb overseas knowledge and weigh 
on its economic growth over the medium to long term.

■ Scope of overseas travel restrictions expanded 
On September 15, the Chinese 
government brought in new rules 
specifying conditions and screening 
procedures for entry and exit. The new 
rules explicitly state that the Ministry of 
Commerce or other departments may 
prohibit Chinese citizens from leaving the 
country if they violate export-control or 
technology import/export regulations and 
are deemed likely to threaten national 
industrial or technological security.  
Under China's Exit and Entry 
Administration Law, which came into 
effect in 2013, defendants and suspects in 
criminal cases, as well as persons involved 
in unsettled civil cases whom courts have 
prohibited from leaving the country, had 
already been subject to exit restrictions. In 
addition, Chinese citizens could be denied 
permission to leave the country if the 
competent departments determined that 
their departure might endanger national 
security or interests. Aside from formal exit 
bans, personnel who handle state secrets 
and officials in the public sector have long 
been subject to controls such as centralized 
passport management and the need to 
obtain approval in advance of overseas travel. What the new rules effectively do is make it clear that exit 
can also be restricted when there are issues from the standpoint of economic security. 
Against this backdrop, it was reported at the end of September that overseas travel restrictions already 
imposed on key personnel in the AI and semiconductor sectors had been expanded to include their spouses, 
children, and other family members. According to these reports, these key personnel include prominent AI 
startup founders, senior executives at major private enterprises, and core R&D talent. Although the measure 
does not constitute a blanket travel ban, affected individuals and their families are reportedly required to 
obtain government approval for exit even in the case of short private trips or business travel. 
This regulatory tightening is thought to be related to the technological rivalry between the U.S. and China. 
As competition surrounding critical technologies such as generative AI intensifies, the Chinese government 
appears to view not only technologies themselves, but also the researchers and engineers who create them, 
as strategic assets. By widening travel curbs to encompass family members, the authorities are likely 
seeking to prevent overseas emigration by individuals or the relocation of R&D operations abroad. A 
noteworthy aspect of the recent reports is that the controls apparently now extend beyond the public sector 
to key personnel at private enterprises, as well as their families. Restrictions like these on talent mobility 
could undermine the mechanisms of technology absorption and productivity improvement that have 
hitherto facilitated China's growth.

### Page 6

ASIA MONTHLY REPORT  November 2026, No.308

The Japan Research Institute, Limited

5 
■ China's productivity gains have been supported by market opening and technology 
absorption 
Productivity exerts a substantial influence on 
economic growth. Total factor productivity (TFP), an 
indicator of an economy's overall production 
efficiency, provides an estimate of efficiency in 
production that cannot be explained solely in terms of 
inputs of capital and labor, and reflects factors such as 
levels of technology and efficiency of resource 
allocation. 
China's TFP has been rising for many years. In 
addition to the transition to a market economy and the 
growth of private enterprises since the country adopted 
the policy of reform and opening up, Chinese 
productivity has benefitted from the absorption of 
foreign technology and managerial know‑how through 
trade and direct investment. A labor shift from 
agriculture to manufacturing and other sectors, along 
with competition among firms and the weeding out of 
weaker players, has also helped deliver productivity 
gains across the economy.  
Nevertheless, China's TFP remains low relative to 
other major economies. According to estimates from 
Penn World Table, China's TFP in 2023 was less than 
half that of the U.S., and it was also below the levels not 
only of Germany and other major European economies 
and of South Korea and Japan, but of Thailand and 
Malaysia, too. Factors behind this include inefficient 
resource allocation and the slow diffusion of advanced 
technologies and management practices. Preferential 
financial and institutional treatment for state‑owned 
enterprises, regional protectionism, and mechanisms 
that impede market entry and exit have distorted the 
allocation of resources, preventing capital and labor 
from being fully utilized by more productive firms and sectors. Moreover, although China has achieved 
strong competitiveness in certain advanced industries, such as electric vehicles and semiconductors, the 
leading-edge technologies and management practices developed in these sectors have not sufficiently 
diffused to the service sector and small and medium‑sized enterprises, which form the foundation of the 
economy, and this has constrained overall productivity growth.  
■ Moves to contain technology could hinder growth over the medium to long term 
Efforts to retain talent in order to prevent technology leakage could further delay China's productivity 
catch‑up with advanced economies. While restrictions on overseas travel by elite professionals may help 
with the retention of domestic R&D talent and protection of current technological advantages, they could 
also reduce opportunities to absorb overseas knowledge and to jointly develop new technologies with 
foreign partners. The transfer of technology and know‑how does not occur solely through the sharing of 
"explicit knowledge," in the form of academic papers, technical blueprints, and so on. "Tacit knowledge," 
which is transmitted through face‑to‑face discussion, collaborative work, and the acquisition of experience 
in production settings, is also crucial. If travel controls delay or prevent participation in international 
conferences, joint research projects, and technology exchanges with overseas companies, opportunities to 
absorb such knowledge may be lost, slowing technological innovation and improvements in production 
processes. Furthermore, if obtaining travel approval becomes more time‑consuming or less predictable, the 
overseas assignment or international deployment of technical personnel could also be obstructed. 
The new rules also cover the entry of foreign nationals. If uncertainty surrounding the administration of 
entry and exit controls increases, it could create obstacles to attracting and retaining highly skilled talent 
from abroad. At a time when inefficient resource allocation and the slow diffusion of advanced knowledge 
are already weighing on productivity growth in China, curbs on people-to- people exchange could further

impede both the absorption and creation of knowledge, as well as its practical application. Although the 
magnitude of the impact will depend on the scope of application and actual implementation on the ground, 
restrictions on talent exchanges and overseas travel that reflect excessive emphasis on technology 
protection could slow China's TFP growth over the medium to long term and act as a headwind to the 
Chinese economy by lowering its potential growth rate. 
(Hiromu Komiya)

2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The Cotality home value index, covering all of Australia, fell
1.2% in October with slight revisions to previous months
again shaving a further 0.3% off prices. The correction
has now seen prices decline 6.7% from their March peak

with annual growth firmly in negative at –2.8%yr. Turnover
continues fall as well, down 22.5%yr. Housing-related
tax policy changes, interest rate rises and heightened
uncertainty have triggered a sharp contraction in markets.
The downturn is now comparable to the previous two
housing market corrections. Nationally, the downturn in
2022-23 saw prices decline 7.5% and turnover fall 29% peak
to trough. The 2017-18 correction saw prices fall 8.6% and

turnover by 24%, with larger falls in Sydney and Melbourne.
Note that unless specified, figures in titles, tables and
commentary are in non‑seasonally adjusted terms, consistent
with the ‘headline’ figures reported by Cotality. However,
figures shown in charts are in seasonally adjusted terms.
Preliminary estimates of turnover show rolling 3mth volumes
down 5.6%qtr in October, previous estimates again revised
lower. The monthly pattern suggests declines re‑accelerated

through September‑October after stabilising somewhat in July‑
August. The RBA's interest rate rise in late September and a
further expected tightening likely contributed to the shift.
The detail shows Brisbane and Perth are now leading the
way in terms of monthly price declines although corrections
to date remain significantly larger in Sydney and Melbourne
while smaller capitals and regional areas continue to see much
milder downturns. Across price tiers, all segments are now

showing similar rates of decline after top price tiers had seen
a more pronounced and earlier downturn. 'Top tier' segments
have tended to lead recent price cycles.
Looking across jurisdictions,
• Sydney prices dropped 1.6% in October having averaged
similar‑sized declines over the previous five months. While
all segments and tiers continue to record falls, the last few
months has seen the pace of decline moderate a touch in the
top tier segments that have led cycles in the past, although

they are still a long way from stabilisation.
• Melbourne prices declined 0.8%mth, in line with
September's move and continuing the slower pace of
retreat. As noted, this likely reflects the weaker starting
point coming into the correction, prices already at 5½ year
lows. Interestingly, the detail shows the moderation in price
declines in recent months has been more pronounced for
'top tier' units where prices are back where they were in
2016.
Housing downturn deepens

3WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
• Brisbane prices are tracking a very fast rate of decline
that is also seeing large downward revisions. Prices fall
2% in October with September and August now both
showing 2.1% falls, revised down from 1.5% and 1.6%

respectively (August having previously been estimated at
just 1%, July's move having seen a similar series of revisions
–1.2% to –1.8%). The combined effect has taken the rolling
3mth change to –6.1%qtr from –4.7%qtr last months and
–2.7%qtr in August and –0.6%qtr in July. At 0.4%yr, annual
price growth has virtually stalled flat having run at 18%yr as
recently as May. All tiers and segments are recording similar
rates of decline.
• Perth is seeing something very similar, prices dropping a

further 1.7% in October, with September's decline revised
down to 2.1% from –1.2% and August's to 2.0% from –1.7%.
At 3.9%yr, annual growth is still positive but off steeply
from the 24% pace back in May. As with Brisbane, all tiers
and segments are recording similar rates of decline.
• Adelaide dwelling prices posted a somewhat milder 1.2%
decline in October, , September's –1.3% decline marked
down to –1.7%. Annual price growth slowed to 3.7%yr,

similar to Perth's but with less of a whiplash from strong
gains to large declines. Turnover has also been much
steadier, sales holding flat over the last three months and
down just 12.4%yr.
• The smaller capitals were again more mixed, price growth
still positive in Darwin (0.6%mth, 9.7%yr), relatively resilient
in Hobart (+0.5%mth, 5.5%yr) but weaker in Canberra
(–0.7%mth, –3.7%yr).
• Regional markets showed a 0.9% decline in Qld but slightly
milder declines in NSW (–0.7%) and Victoria (0.6%). Annual

prices growth is in the 1.4‑3.5%yr range.
All up, the October updates make for bleak reading. With the
RBA widely expected to raise interest rates again, the near
term outlook remains very challenging. Next week's consumer
sentiment update will show how this is shaping housing‑
related sentiment – so far, consumer price expectations have
weakened but stopped short of swinging to the clear negative
consensus that emerged in the 2022‑23 correction. That now

looks to be at risk. Either way, momentum looks set to remain
weak into year‑end and, barring a significantly improved
inflation and interest rate outlook, well into 2027.

PROPRIETARY RESEARCH FROM LSEG 
THIS WEEK IN EARNINGS

This Week in Earnings provides analysis and commentary on aggregate  
earnings estimate revisions, growth rates and valuations.

ANALYST: Tajinder Dhillon, CFA October 30, 2026 
tajinder.dhillon@lseg.com; 
THIS WEEK IN EARNINGS 
AGGREGATE ESTIMATES AND REVISIONS 
 26Q3 Y/Y earnings are expected to be 31.6%. Excluding the energy sector, the Y/Y earnings estimate is 26.4%. 
 Of the 299 companies in the S&P 500 that have reported earnings to date for 26Q3, 81.6% have reported earnings 
above analyst estimates. This compares to a long-term average of 67.7% and prior four quarter average of 81.5%. 
 26Q3 Y/Y revenue is expected to be 13.6%. Excluding the energy sector, the growth estimate is 12.2%. 
 69.9% of companies have reported 26Q3 revenue above analyst expectations. This compares to a long-term 
average of 63% and an average over the past four quarters of 76.8%. 
 For 26Q3, there have been 40 negative EPS preannouncements issued by S&P 500 corporations compared to 66 
positive EPS preannouncements. By dividing 40 by 66 the N/P ratio is 0.6 for the S&P 500 Index.  
 The forward four-quarter (26Q4– 27Q3) P/E ratio for the S&P 500 is 19.0. 
 During the week of Nov. 2, 144 S&P 500 companies are expected to report quarterly earnings.

This Week in Earnings provides analysis and commentary on aggregate earnings estimate revisions, growth rates and valuations.  2 
There has been an increase in the share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 since the start of the quarter (to $778.4B 
from $760.7B). Three of the eleven sectors have experienced downward revisions to estimates.

Since Aug. 1, the communication services (-20.5%) and materials (-7%) sectors have recorded the highest percentage 
decreases in earnings, while the energy (33%) sector has recorded the highest percentage increase in earnings. 
Overall, share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 have increased by 2.3% since the start of the quarter.

Since Aug. 1, the communication services (-14.7B) and materials (-1.2B) sectors have recorded the highest dollar-level 
decreases in earnings, while the energy (15.7B) sector has recorded the highest dollar-level increase in earnings. 
Overall, expected share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 have increased by 17.7B since the start of the quarter.

299 companies in the S&P 500 Index have reported earnings for Q3 2026. Of these companies, 81.6% reported 
earnings above analyst expectations and 15.7% reported earnings below analyst expectations. In a typical quarter 
(since 1994), 67% of companies beat estimates and 20% miss estimates. Over the past four quarters, 82% of 
companies beat the estimates and 15% missed estimates. 
In aggregate, companies are reporting earnings that are 4.5% above estimates, which compares to a long-term (since 
1994) average surprise factor of 4.4% and the average surprise factor over the prior four quarters of 7.7%.

299 companies in the S&P 500 Index have reported revenue for Q3 2026. Of these companies, 69.9% reported 
revenue above analyst expectations and 30.1% reported revenue below analyst expectations. In a typical quarter 
(since 2002), 63% of companies beat estimates and 37% miss estimates. Over the past four quarters, 77% of 
companies beat the estimates and 23% missed estimates. 
In aggregate, companies are reporting revenues that are 2.4% above estimates, which compares to a long-term (since 
2002) average surprise factor of 1.3% and the average surprise factor over the prior four quarters of 2.5%.

The estimated earnings growth rate for the S&P 500 for 26Q3 is 31.6%. If the energy sector is excluded, the growth 
rate declines to 26.4%. The S&P 500 expects to see share-weighted earnings of $778.4B in 26Q3, compared to share-
weighted earnings of $591.4B (based on the year-ago earnings of the current 503 constituents) in 25Q3. 
11 of the 11 sectors in the index expect to see an improvement in earnings relative to 25Q3. The Energy and 
Information Technology sectors have the highest earnings growth rates for the quarter, while the Consumer 
Discretionary sector has the weakest anticipated growth compared to 25Q3. 
The Energy sector has the highest earnings growth rate (146.2%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $63B in 26Q3, 
compared to earnings of $25.6B in 25Q3. 4 of the 5 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see higher earnings 
than a year ago. The Oil & Gas Refining & Marketing (566.5%) and Integrated Oil & Gas (143.8%) sub-industries have 
the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate declines to 39%. 
The Information Technology sector has the second highest earnings growth rate (68.9%) of any sector. It is expected 
to earn $260.2B in 26Q3, compared to earnings of $154B in 25Q3. 12 of the 12 sub-industries in the sector are 
anticipated to see higher earnings than a year ago. The Semiconductors (137%) and Semiconductor Materials & 
Equipment (71.5%) sub-industries have the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, 
the growth rate declines to 28.2%. 
The Consumer Discretionary sector has the lowest earnings growth rate (3.9%) of any sector. It is expected to earn 
$56.3B in 26Q3, compared to earnings of $54.2B in 25Q3. 7 of the 18 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to 
see lower earnings than a year ago. The Casinos & Gaming (-44.1%) and Automotive Parts & Equipment (-38.8%) 
sub-industries have the lowest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate 
improves to 4.5%.

rate declines to 25%. The S&P 500 expects to see share-weighted earnings of $810.7B in 26Q4, compared to share-
weighted earnings of $625.1B (based on the year-ago earnings of the current 503 constituents) in 25Q4. 
11 of the 11 sectors in the index expect to see an improvement in earnings relative to 25Q4. The Energy and 
Information Technology sectors have the highest earnings growth rates for the quarter, while the Industrials sector has 
the weakest anticipated growth compared to 25Q4. 
The Energy sector has the highest earnings growth rate (145.6%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $59.5B in 26Q4, 
compared to earnings of $24.2B in 25Q4. 3 of the 5 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see higher earnings 
than a year ago. The Oil & Gas Refining & Marketing (366.3%) and Integrated Oil & Gas (175.7%) sub-industries have 
the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate declines to 52.1%. 
The Information Technology sector has the second highest earnings growth rate (56.3%) of any sector. It is expected 
to earn $292.1B in 26Q4, compared to earnings of $186.8B in 25Q4. 12 of the 12 sub-industries in the sector are

the growth rate declines to 19.1%. 
The Industrials sector has the lowest earnings growth rate (3.9%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $52B in 26Q4, 
compared to earnings of $50.1B in 25Q4. 2 of the 20 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see lower earnings 
than a year ago. The Aerospace & Defense (-39.6%) and Passenger Airlines (-29.8%) sub-industries have the lowest 
earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate improves to 30.5%.

improves to 6%.

STARMINE EARNINGS SURPRISE FORECAST
Looking forward at quarterly performance, we use StarMine’s SmartEstimate® from LSEG to determine which
companies in the S&P 500 are better poised to beat and miss earnings estimates. The SmartEstimate® is a weighted

average of analyst estimates, with more weight given to more recent estimates and more accurate analysts. Our
studies have shown that when the SmartEstimate® differs significantly from the consensus (I/B/E/S mean estimate),
the Predicted Surprise accurately predicts the direction of earnings surprises or further revisions 70% of the time.
When a significant Predicted Surprise for revenue is also present for the period, the accuracy improves to 78%.

StarMine ARM from LSEG is an analyst revisions stock ranking model, designed to predict future changes in analyst
sentiment. ARM incorporates more accurate earnings estimates through the SmartEstimate prediction service. ARM
region rankings scores companies by region on a scale of 1 to 100 where 100 represents the most bullish sentiment.
Over the next two weeks, 147 S&P 500 companies are expected to report earnings. Of these companies, 17 positive

surprises and 11 negative surprises are expected from S&P 500 companies reporting quarterly results.

## Document 361: NZ Q3 Labour market data - Potential RBNZ implications (2026-11-14)

Source PDF: all report pdf/NZ Q3 Labour market data - Potential RBNZ implications (2026-11-14).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-14
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#labour #newzealand
NZ Q3 Labour market data - Potential RBNZ implications
By Kelly Eckhold
Chief Economist, Westpac New Zealand
13:16 November 04 2026 Share
The Q3 labour market statistics were weaker than expected. We discuss potential
implications for the RBNZ noting that a December hike remains more likely than not at this
point.
Q3 Labour market data: potential RBNZ implications
The Q3 labour market data were weaker than RBNZ and our expectations.

### Page 2

Today's labour market data seem unequivocally weaker than expected by either us or the RBNZ. While
likely not a gamechanger for the cycle overall, it will add ammunition to the arguments of the MPC
doves that a pause in interest rate rises will make sense soon - perhaps in December or in the New
Year. It’s likely the case that a bit much was priced into December, so the change in market
probabilities in the wake of today’s data makes sense - although my base case remains that a
December rate rise remains more likely than not.
The message from the RBNZ in the October meeting though was to be careful about pricing in "done
deals" and that the outlook will be data-dependent. As we noted at the time, alongside measures of
inflation expectations, the key domestic data to watch ahead of the RBNZ’s 9 December meeting will
be indicators of what is happening to excess capacity (the output gap) as this will be a key determinant
of the Bank’s assessment of the outlook for inflation over the coming year.
It’s useful to remember in the RBNZ’s forecasting framework that GDP figures are the main
determinant of excess capacity in the economy (i.e., the output gap). The Q2 GDP data told us that the
output gap probably narrowed a bit through Q2, mainly reflecting historical revisions. In light of today’s
labour market numbers, the forecast for growth in Q3 may have some downside risk - but there are
conflicting indicators there, so it’s not that clear. However, it’s probably hard to argue that the level of
excess capacity reduced further in Q3 given today’s data. 
In the eyes of dovish MPC members, the rise in the unemployment rate and other measures important
for the RBNZ's labour market dashboard likely push in the direction of having greater comfort with the
medium-term inflation outlook. The vacancy to unemployment ratio increased a bit, labour constraints
eased a bit, and the unemployment rate has increased. Hence from a labour market perspective it
could be argued that policy could have more time all else equal. The outcome of the election, with the
possibility of a new RBNZ mandate, could increase the prominence of these factors - although this
won’t likely be an explicit factor in the RBNZ’s thinking in December even though it may be in the minds
of individual MPC members.
It could be the case that the argument for increases in the OCR track through 2027 could be
undermined with this data. It’s important to note (probably self-evidently) that there are other data that
will also be relevant. Hence the risk of a pause earlier in the New Year has probably increased with this
data, all else equal. I don't think our forecast peak OCR is really at issue here, but the time taken to get
there could be more flexible. Takes along the lines of "3% is the terminal rate" are very premature and
misplaced, in our view.

A December hike remains more likely than not, but there will be a clear debate to be had between the
MPC hawks and doves.
The forecast peak OCR is likely less impacted than the time to reach that peak. Further pauses in the
tightening cycle could come earlier than currently forecast. 
The outlook for the exchange rate is especially clouded by this data should it take a longer time for
NZ rates to move closer to global norms. 
Watch for updated RBNZ commentary on 10 and 19 November. The Governor’s observation that it
would have been useful to update markets ahead of the October meeting could imply a greater
willingness to comment in the coming weeks.

### Page 3

The outlook for the exchange rate is undermined by this data. I feel very happy about our year-end
target of 79c for the NZD/AUD rate with the risk that a recovery in the NZD takes longer than currently
assumed.
Looking ahead, there are some opportunities for the RBNZ to update markets on their views. Top of
mind is Paul Conway's 10 November speech to the UBS Australasia Conference. The Governor is also
speaking to the Institute of Directors on 19 November. If the RBNZ has something to say, then it might
happen in conjunction with these events (the 10 November speech is titled “How is the NZ economy
tracking”, so it will be difficult for Conway to avoid saying something about how he is interpreting
today’s data). We also note the Governor's comments in the October MPR that it could have been
useful to have been more flexible about updating markets on emerging views of new economic data.
This might increase the likelihood that these events could be used to provide guidance on the RBNZ’s
views of this data (and other emerging events), especially if it feels that the market’s interpretation is at
odds with its own.

### Page 2

The Week Ahead November 6, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         2

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

Taiwan

Export growth is expected to ease to 
44.4% yoy in October from 60.9% in 
September. A higher comparison base 
makes exceptional export growth 
difficult to sustain from 4Q onwards. The 
global AI cycle is also expected to settle 
into a more normal pace into 2027, as 
higher interest rates lift capital costs 
and US mid-term election results 
heighten regulatory uncertainty. These 
factors underpin our forecast for 
Taiwan’s GDP growth to return to high 
single digits from 4Q onwards and into 
2027.

Malaysia

We expect Malaysia’s industrial 
production growth to rise to 5.5% yoy in 
September 2026 from 5.0% yoy in 
August. The uptick was likely driven by 
export-oriented manufacturing as 
strong artificial intelligence (AI)-related 
demand continued to support growth in 
electrical and electronics goods, 
alongside a smaller contraction in mining.

China

Export growth is expected to decelerate 
from 36.3% yoy in September to 28.1% in 
October. Both the official manufacturing 
PMI and new export orders sub-index fell 
below 50 after two months in expansion 
territory. High-frequency trade indicators 
also point to slowing momentum, with 
average daily deadweight tonnage handled 
at China’s 20 major ports falling from 7.2% 
yoy in September to -4.2% in October. 
Freight activity weakened as well, with 
growth in international cargo flights 
dropping from 3.6% yoy to -5.9% over the 
same period. On the price front, factory-gate 
and raw material price pressures moderated, 
with the main raw material purchasing price 
and producer price sub-indices declining 
from 60.8 and 54.0 to 56.8 and 51.7, 
respectively. Despite remaining above the 
50 threshold, the moderation suggests some 
easing in pipeline price pressures. 
Downstream consumer price pressures are 
also likely to remain subdued amid soft 
domestic demand. As a result, CPI inflation 
is expected to ease slightly from 0.9% yoy in 
September to 0.8% in October.

India

We expect headline inflation to rise to 6.0% 
yoy in October from 5.5% the month before, 
on higher food costs and a low base effect. 
High frequency data pointed to a further 
pick-up in sequential pressures in food 
segments, across cereals, edible oil, 
perishables (vegetables) etc. Non-food 
components should find relief from further 
moderation in precious metals and delay in 
passage of retail fuel price changes. Core 
inflation is nonetheless set to quicken to 4.5-
4.6% as services notch increases and as 
indirect tax effects in the base, fall out of the 
equation. With inflation expected to test 
past the higher end of the RBI target range, 
we expect a second successive rate hike in 
December. The decline in oil prices has also 
led to cooling of overnight OIS rates as 
concerns over aggressive rate hikes have 
eased. Goods trade deficit is likely to stay 
wide at above $30bn in Oct.

## Document 410: US MARKETS CALL_ S&P 500 Valuation Dives As Earnings Soar

Source PDF: all report pdf/US MARKETS CALL_ S&P 500 Valuation Dives As Earnings Soar.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-07
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: US MARKETS CALL: S&P 500 Valuation Dives As Earnings Soar
Date:    5 hours ago
US MARKETS CALL: S&P 500 Valuation Dives As Earnings Soar

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
US MARKETS CALL: S&P 500
Valuation Dives As Earnings
Soar
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 7 Nov 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Baptiste Azais / Unsplash
The stock market dived last week. Investors priced in political backlash
against data centers after Democrats swept Congress. We anticipated
buying opportunities after the midterm elections, and Thursday’s selloff
delivered some. This remains an earnings-led bull market. Valuations are
falling while earnings are soaring. We still view the AI backlash as another

### Page 3

speed bump, not a roadblock. We are sticking with our 7,900 year-end
target for the S&P 500.
I. Performance
The Nasdaq entered correction territory on Thursday and closed the week
below its 200-day moving average (chart). It has done this before recently.
It rebounded sharply after its Spring 2025 "Liberation Day" plunge and
again after the Iran war started earlier this year, quickly resuming its
uptrend both times.
The equal-weight S&P 500 is holding up remarkably well. It remains near

its recent record high and above both its 50-day and 200-day moving
averages (chart). The damage has been concentrated in the AI trade, not
the broader market.

### Page 4

The Magnificent-7 are underperforming again. The MAGS ETF is up only
2.4% ytd, versus 7.8% for the S&P 500 (chart). The Impressive-493 also
took a hit this week. The XMAGS ETF includes Micron and the other
semiconductor stocks hit hardest in the selloff. It is still up 10.0% ytd.
Dow Theory remains bullish. The Dow Jones Industrial Average is holding
up well near its highs (chart). The Transports have lagged. They should
improve if oil prices continue to fall.

II. Earnings
Earnings season picked up this week. The blended Q3 S&P 500 EPS
growth estimate (i.e., actual results for companies that have reported,
estimates for those that haven’t yet) rose to 25.9% y/y from 23.8% a week
earlier, helped by AMD’s solid results. Q4’s estimate edged down to 29.7%

### Page 5

(chart). As we noted previously, Q2’s 50.4% y/y EPS growth rate was
boosted by mark-to-market gains at Alphabet and Amazon. Excluding
those, growth was closer to 25%, which is still very strong.
Forward earnings rose to a record $418.25. It is converging on the 2027
estimate of $426.28, which continues to climb (chart). Analysts’ 2028
consensus S&P 500 EPS estimate rose to $495.14; it’s been closing in on
$500—which is our target for 2030!  We are bullish, but not as bullish as

the analysts. The drop in the forward P/E this year suggests that investors
also believe analysts are too bullish on earnings.
Forward earnings for the S&P 500, S&P 400, and S&P 600 all rose to
record highs again last week (chart). Earnings strength remains broad.

### Page 6

S&P 500 forward revenues per share is up 15.9% y/y, while forward
earnings per share is up 39.0% y/y (chart).
III. Valuation
The S&P 500 forward P/E fell to 17.6 last week, below the 2025 Liberation
Day low (chart). P/E plunges typically accompany corrections and
recessions. The economy shows no sign of a recession anytime soon.
Investors instead fear irrational exuberance in earnings forecasts.

### Page 7

Since the bull market began on October 12, 2022, the S&P 500 has risen
106.2%. Forward earnings is up 75.5%, while the forward P/E is up 22.5%
(chart). The P/E and E both drove the rally until the forward P/E peaked in
October last year. Since then, E has done all the work. Investors are
finding it harder to predict the return on all the AI capital spending. They
won’t pay an excessive multiple given that uncertainty.
The S&P 500 Semiconductors industry accounts for much of the P/E drop.

The industry index's forward P/E has plunged to 13.1, well below the S&P
500’s (chart). Semis are now cheaper than they were during the 2022
bear market.

### Page 8

IV. Fixed Income
The futures market expects about two rate hikes over the next 12 months.
That explains why the 2-year Treasury yield remains well above the 3.88%
federal funds rate, even after easing to 4.46% following the midterms
(chart).
The 10-year Treasury yield continues to fall. It dropped to 4.84% and is
testing its uptrend line from last year’s low (chart). We’ll see if it bounces.
Either way, it is back in the 4.00%-5.00% “old normal” range where we feel

comfortable.

### Page 9

The 10-year TIPS yield fell to 2.62%. The yield spread between the 10-
year Treasury and TIPS, a widely used proxy for expected inflation, eased
to 2.22% (chart). That proxy tends to move with oil prices. The recent drop
in crude showed up here.
V. Commodities
The price of Brent crude oil fell to $98.32 a barrel in recent days (chart).
No news of the re-escalation of the war in the Middle East has emerged
since the midterms. Hopes for a US-Iran deal are reflected in the oil

market. We still see a risk of re-escalation.

### Page 10

Nearby gasoline futures dropped to $2.78 a gallon last week (chart). If
sustained, that should start to show up as lower inflation in a couple of
months. Renewed fighting in the Middle East would reverse that.
Steel prices show no sign of a slowdown in data center construction. US
Midwest hot-rolled coil rose to $1,357 a ton, the highest since 2022
(chart).

### Page 11

Agricultural prices have eased. The S&P GSCI Agriculture Spot index fell
to 376.77 last week (chart). Fears of a food price spike should subside.
💡

log in to the Yardeni QuickTakes website and post them at the end
of the QuickTakes article. Paid members' contributions may be
featured in our segment, "Ed Answers Your Questions".

### Page 12

clause.

## Document 424: Who finances private credit in the United States_ What the Federal Reserve’s data reveal (2026-10-28)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Who finances private credit in the United States_ What the Federal Reserve’s data reveal (2026-10-28).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-28
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

28 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
WHO FINANCES PRIVATE CREDIT IN THE UNITED STATES? WHAT THE FEDERAL RESERVE’S DATA REVEAL
Céline Choulet
SOURCE: FEDERAL RESERVE (ENHANCED FINANCIAL ACCOUNTS), BNP PARIBAS
The private credit market raises many questions, and its lack of transparency is not helping. Official public data remain scarce. The available
estimates come mainly from private sources, whose methodologies vary widely. The Financial Accounts of the Federal Reserve (Fed) now shed

new light on the matter. They reveal that private credit finances 7.1% of US corporate debt through households (31% of funds’ total financing),
insurers and pension funds (20%), and banks (19%).
Private credit can take several forms. The most common is loans granted by closed-end funds 1 to small and medium-sized, unlisted and highly
indebted companies. These loans are generally variable-rate, with flexible terms based on cash flow rather than a measure of profitability. While

this asset class has inherent vulnerabilities 2 (Bank of France, 2025), it is also particularly well-suited to financing innovative and long-term
projects (such as digital 3, infrastructure, renewable energy and healthcare), especially when combined with an equity stake in the company. Its
slower growth in Europe likely explains, in part, the region’s lag in innovation financing.
Private credit finances 7.1% of US corporate debt…
Private credit has grown rapidly in the United States over the past decade and has established itself as an alternative to bank loans for

a category of borrowers. It now accounts for 17.3% of the total outstanding loans to American non-financial corporations (NFCs) 4 and 7.1%
1 Strictly speaking, a closed-end fund has a fixed term during which investors cannot withdraw their invested capital. However, the sector has diversified since 2020. The Fed distinguishes
between 1) strictly closed-end private funds (private debt funds), 2) business development companies, which may have an indefinite lifespan and allow for occasional redemptions of

shares, and 3) interval and tender offer funds, which offer the possibility of scheduled redemptions.
2 Notably, high credit risk, high leverage, difficulty in valuing loans, opaque credit ratings, occasionally complex credit structures, high concentration within the sector and increasing
liquidity risk.
3 In 2025, nearly half of the direct loans made by private credit funds went to the tech sector (software, data centres and artificial intelligence) (Abbassi et al., 2026).

4 No comparable data exist for the Eurozone. According to France Invest (2026) data, private credit issued by French funds accounted for 4% of annual bank credit flows to NFCs in 2025.

of their debt. Approximately one-third (35%) is managed by strictly closed-end funds, with another third (36%) by semi-open-end funds

… through banks, insurers and pension funds, and households

In yet another example of the lengthening of the credit intermediation chain in the United States (following that of the mortgage market),
banks are among the main creditors of private credit funds (65% of their debt and 19% of their total financing). The low risk weighting
of loans granted by banks to these vehicles, compared to that of direct bank loans to non-financial corporations, is undoubtedly a factor
(Chernenko et al., 2024). The holdings of insurers and pension funds – consisting mainly of shares in strictly closed-end funds – make

them among the leading investors in private debt funds (30% of issued shares and 20% of total financing). This asset class offers them the
opportunity to diversify their portfolios and attractive returns. The rise of semi-open-end funds has expanded the investor base to include
retail investors (31% of total financing), albeit at the cost of increased liquidity risk, as evidenced by the significant wave of redemptions in
late 2025 and early 2026 5. American households directly hold 70% of the equity issued by semi-open funds and 22% of the shares issued

by strictly closed funds 6.
